Result: Two intermediate phases are identified between dimer and Néel phases: a mixed plaquette-dimer (MPD) phase and a spiral phase. The MPD phase shows strong intradimer correlations and weak tetramerization on empty plaquettes; transitions from MPD to dimer and spiral phases are first order.

Conclusion: Combining results with known S=1/2 and classical (S→∞) boundaries yields a global S-g phase diagram that systematically shows the suppression of quantum effects with increasing spin, providing a theoretical framework for larger-S Shastry-Sutherland lattice materials.

Abstract: We investigate the ground-state phase diagram of the $S=1$ and $S=3/2$ Heisenberg model on the two-dimensional Shastry-Sutherland lattice (SSL) using density matrix renormalization group (DMRG) and cluster mean-field theory (CMFT). Between the dimer phase and Néel antiferromagnetic phases, we identify two intermediate phases: a mixed plaquette-dimer (MPD) phase and a spiral phase. These phases are characterized via bond energies and spin-spin correlation functions; phase boundaries are located from the ground-state energy derivative and entanglement entropy. The MPD phase exhibits strong intradimer correlations and weak tetramerization on the empty plaquettes, and its transitions to the dimer and spiral phases are first order. Combining our results with the known boundaries for $S=1/2$ and the classical limit $S\to\infty$, we construct a global $S$-$g$ phase diagram. This diagram reveals the progressive suppression of quantum effects with increasing $S$ and offers a theoretical framework for larger-$S$ SSL materials.

</details>


### [95] [Superconfined Antiferromagnons on the Two-Dimensional Penrose Lattice](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.24430)
*Takashi Inoue,Shoji Yamamoto*

Main category: cond-mat.str-el

TL;DR: This paper discovers confined spin-wave states in the 2D Penrose Heisenberg antiferromagnet, which are localized to tricoordinated sites due to site potentials from Coulomb interactions, unlike the more extended confined states in the tight-binding model. These states are robust against 1/S corrections and exhibit a novel "superconfinement" effect from emergent O(S⁰) interactions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate whether confined states exist in the spin-wave spectrum of the antiferromagnetic Heisenberg model on the quasiperiodic Penrose lattice, and to understand how they differ from the well-known confined electronic states in the tight-binding model.

Method: The authors study the spin-S nearest-neighbor antiferromagnetic Heisenberg model on the 2D Penrose lattice using linear spin-wave theory (1/S expansion). They analyze the eigenstates, identify confined modes, classify their building blocks (four types for spin waves vs. six for electrons), and examine 1/S corrections.

Result: Massively degenerate confined spin-wave states exist strictly at tricoordinated sites (unlike tight-binding confined states which extend to both tri- and pentacoordinated sites). Only four types of confined building blocks appear (vs. six in tight-binding). Confined states are robust under 1/S corrections. Emergent O(S⁰) interactions cause further "superconfinement" into two separate groups within tricoordinated sites.

Conclusion: Confined spin waves are fundamentally different from confined electronic states in the same lattice due to the site potentials originating from Coulomb interactions. They are more localized (only tricoordinated sites) and exhibit superconfinement, suggesting novel magnetic phenomena in quasiperiodic systems.

Abstract: We find novel confined states in the spin-$S$ nearest-neighbor antiferromagnetic Heisenberg model on the two-dimensional Penrose lattice. Linear spin waves have massively degenerate eigenstates strictly confined to tricoordinated sites. They contrast with the well-known itinerant analogs in the tight-binding model, where electrons are confined but extended to both tricoordinated and pentacoordinated sites. It is the site potentials in the spin-wave Hamiltonian, originating from Coulomb interactions between electrons, that confine spin waves to minimally coordinated sites only. Confined states in the tight-binding Hamiltonian consist of six types of building blocks, whereas those in the spin-wave Hamiltonian consist of only four of them. Confined spin waves are robust against $1/S$ corrections. Emergent $O(S^{0})$ interactions further confine -- superconfine -- spin waves into two separate groups within tricoordinated sites.

</details>


### [96] [Emergent Dispersive Multipolar Excitations in NaErSe$_{2}$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.24527)
*Zheng Zhang,Mingfang Shu,Mingtai Xie,Weizhen Zhuo,Yanzhen Cai,Christian Balz,Jianting Ji,Feng Jin,Jie Ma*

Main category: cond-mat.str-el

TL;DR: NaErSe₂ exhibits emergent coupling between local CEF excitations and collective magnon modes, with symmetry-selected dispersions and field-tunable multipolar bands.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate how local and collective excitations, typically decoupled in condensed-matter systems, can become coupled in strongly spin-orbit coupled frustrated magnets.

Method: Neutron spectroscopy combined with total angular momentum modeling on the triangular antiferromagnet NaErSe₂.

Result: Symmetry-selected dispersions where magnon branches from the Γ₄ ground state are replicated on higher Γ₄ levels but couple with Γ₅,₆ levels to form distinct multipolar bands; an applied magnetic field reconstructs CEF wavefunctions and polarizes the system into a multipolar ferromagnet.

Conclusion: Demonstrates emergent coupling of local and collective excitations driven by strong spin-orbit coupling, establishing NaErSe₂ as a platform for field-tunable multipolar excitations.

Abstract: In most condensed-matter systems, local and collective excitations remain decoupled due to their distinct energy scales. Here, we identify coupled local-collective excitations in the triangular antiferromagnet NaErSe$_2$ by combining neutron spectroscopy with total angular momentum modeling. The low-lying crystalline electric field (CEF) doublets include a dipolar $Γ_4$ ground state forming stripe-$x$ order and a $Γ_{5,6}$ excited state with dipole-octupole character. High-resolution spectra reveal emergent symmetry-selected dispersions, where magnon branches from the ground state are replicated on higher $Γ_4$ levels but couple with the $Γ_{5,6}$ levels to form a distinct multipolar band. An applied magnetic field reconstructs the CEF wavefunctions and polarizes the system into a multipolar ferromagnet, further reshaping the spectra. This study demonstrates the emergent coupling of local and collective excitations driven by strong spin-orbit coupling and establishes NaErSe$_2$ as a platform for field-tunable multipolar excitations in frustrated magnets.

</details>


### [97] [Analytic Origin of Green-Function Compression in the Intermediate Representation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.24814)
*Takahiro Misawa*

Main category: cond-mat.str-el

TL;DR: This paper reveals that the intermediate representation (IR) basis for compressing imaginary-time Green functions in quantum many-body physics has a finite-Laplace-transform structure, whose eigenfunctions expand naturally in oblate spheroidal wave functions, providing the first analytic explanation for its compression capability.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The IR basis is widely used for compressing imaginary-time Green functions in quantum many-body calculations, but the analytic origin of its compression capability has remained unclear.

Method: The authors uncover a finite-Laplace-transform structure underlying the IR kernel and show that the eigenfunctions admit a natural expansion in terms of oblate spheroidal wave functions, a class of classical special functions.

Result: The finite-Laplace-transform structure analytically clarifies the compression mechanism of the IR basis, explaining the rapid decay of singular values and weak growth of effective rank with inverse temperature.

Conclusion: The results provide a mathematical foundation for compressing imaginary-time Green functions, bridging quantum many-body physics with information compression theory and finite integral transforms.

Abstract: Information compression plays a central role in diverse fields of modern science and technology, from communication theory to machine learning. In condensed-matter physics, the intermediate representation (IR) basis has recently been developed as an efficient method for compressing imaginary-time Green functions, which are fundamental quantities for describing quantum many-body systems. This compression relies on the rapid decay of the singular values with the basis index and the unusually weak growth of the effective rank with inverse temperature. Because of these useful features, the IR basis is now widely used as a standard method in quantum many-body calculations. However, the analytic origin of its compression capability has remained unclear. Here we uncover a finite-Laplace-transform structure underlying the IR kernel, which reveals that the eigenfunctions of the IR kernel admit a natural expansion in terms of classical special functions, the oblate spheroidal wave functions. This finite-Laplace-transform structure also enables us to analytically clarify the compression mechanism of the IR basis. Our results provide a mathematical foundation for the compression of imaginary-time Green functions, connecting quantum many-body physics with theories of information compression and finite integral transforms.

</details>


### [98] [Revisiting spin Hamiltonian parameters in a Kitaev material via Bayesian optimization of magnetization curves](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.24857)
*Takahiro Misawa,Ryo Tamura,Kazuyoshi Yoshimi,Youhei Yamaji*

Main category: cond-mat.str-el

TL;DR: The authors present a data-driven approach using Bayesian optimization to determine the spin Hamiltonian parameters of α-RuCl₃ by fitting experimental magnetization curves, finding optimal parameters (K, Γ, Γ', J, g_c) = (-6.0, 7.5, -0.3, -1.75, 2.3) meV, and demonstrating that the cost function is insensitive to |K|, weakly dependent on Γ' and J, while favoring a large positive Γ.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Standard ab initio approaches for determining spin Hamiltonian parameters can be highly sensitive to methodological details, especially in candidate Kitaev materials like α-RuCl₃. An alternative, more reliable method is needed to accurately determine magnetic Hamiltonian parameters.

Method: Bayesian optimization of experimental magnetization curves along b- and c-axis directions was used to optimize five spin Hamiltonian parameters (K, Γ, Γ', J, g_c). Additional validation was performed by computing static spin structure factor, magnetic susceptibility, and specific heat.

Result: The optimal parameter set is (K, Γ, Γ', J, g_c) = (-6.0, 7.5, -0.3, -1.75, 2.3) meV. The cost function is insensitive to the absolute value of K, depends weakly on Γ' and J, but favors a large positive Γ. Computed observables favor the large-Γ scenario over the small-g_c scenario.

Conclusion: Bayesian optimization combined with accurate low-energy solvers provides an effective, systematic approach for determining spin Hamiltonian parameters of quantum magnets directly from experimental data, circumventing the methodological sensitivity issues of ab initio calculations.

Abstract: Determining the spin Hamiltonian of a magnetic compound is crucial for understanding its magnetic properties. A standard approach is to derive model parameters from $ab$ $initio$ calculations based on the crystal structure. However, the resulting Hamiltonian can depend sensitively on methodological details of the $ab$ $initio$ procedure. This issue is particularly evident in $α$-RuCl$_3$, a candidate Kitaev material. Here, we present an alternative, data-driven approach to determine the spin Hamiltonian parameters of $α$-RuCl$_3$ by Bayesian optimization of experimental magnetization curves along the $b$- and $c$-axis directions. We optimize five parameters, namely the Kitaev interaction $K$, off-diagonal interactions $Γ$ and $Γ'$, the Heisenberg interaction $J$, and the $c$-axis $g$-factor $g_c$. The parameter set that minimizes the cost function is $(K,Γ,Γ',J,g_c)=(-6.0,\,7.5,\,-0.3,\,-1.75,\,2.3)$, where the exchange couplings are in meV. We find that the cost function is insensitive to the absolute value of the Kitaev coupling $K$. Thus, the magnetization data alone do not determine its energy scale. The cost function also depends only weakly on $Γ'$ and $J$, while the optimization favors a large positive $Γ$. By computing the static spin structure factor, magnetic susceptibility, and specific heat, we show that these quantities favor the large-$Γ$ scenario over the small-$g_c$ scenario and that the parameter set that minimizes the cost function yields good agreement with experiment. The combination of Bayesian optimization and accurate low-energy solvers provides an effective approach for determining parameters of spin Hamiltonians. This methodology opens a systematic route to determining spin Hamiltonians in quantum magnets from experimental data.

</details>


### [99] [Strong Correlation Effect and In-gap State in the Doped Electron-Hole Two-Band Hubbard Model Based on the Dynamical Mean-Field Theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.24927)
*Masashi Akiyama,Yusuke Inokuma,Yoshiaki Ono*

Main category: cond-mat.str-el

TL;DR: Using DMFT, the paper shows that the spinless two-band Hubbard model undergoes a first-order Lifshitz transition from a correlated semimetal (large effective mass) to a band insulator as interband Coulomb interaction U increases. At U > Uc, slight hole doping (~0.01) creates a remarkable correlated semimetal with in-gap states, similar to doped Mott insulators.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate the strong correlation effect in the spinless electron-hole two-band Hubbard model, particularly the role of interband Coulomb interaction and doping on the semimetal-to-insulator transition.

Method: Dynamical mean-field theory (DMFT) applied to the spinless electron-hole two-band Hubbard model.

Result: At half filling, both renormalization factor Z and conduction electron number n_c decrease with U, reaching Z~0.15 and n_c~0.1 at U_c~bandwidth, where a first-order Lifshitz transition occurs. Slight hole doping (x~0.01) in the band insulator (U>U_c) yields a correlated semimetal with Z~0.1 and in-gap states that grow with x.

Conclusion: The system exhibits a first-order Lifshitz transition from correlated semimetal to band insulator at critical U, and slight doping can induce a remarkable correlated semimetal with in-gap states reminiscent of doped Mott insulators.

Abstract: We investigate the strong correlation effect in the spinless electron-hole two-band Hubbard model using the dynamical mean-field theory. At half filling, both the renormalization factor $Z$ and the number of conduction electrons (valence holes) $n_c$ decrease with increasing the interband Coulomb interaction $U$ down to $Z\sim 0.15$ and $n_c\sim 0.1$ for $U_c \sim \mbox{bandwidth}$ at which the first-order Lifshitz transition occurs from a correlated semimetal with a large effective mass $m^*/m=Z^{-1}$ to a band insulator with a finite gap due to the Hartree shift. A slight hole doping $x$ in the band insulator with a large $U>U_c$ yields a remarkable correlated semimetal with $Z\sim 0.1$ at $x\sim 0.01$, where in-gap states emerge above the valence band top and those weights increase with increasing $x$ together with the increase in $Z$ similar to the in-gap states observed in doped Mott insulators.

</details>


### [100] [Dynamical susceptibility and quantum Fisher information in the Su-Schrieffer-Heeger model with Hatsugai-Kohmoto interactions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.25231)
*Sepide Mohamadi,Jahanfar Abouie*

Main category: cond-mat.str-el

TL;DR: The paper studies dynamical spin/charge susceptibilities and quantum Fisher information in the SSH-HK model, showing that HK interactions yield qualitatively distinct response behavior compared to Hubbard-type interactions, and that quantum Fisher information can probe filling sectors but remains insensitive to topological transitions within uniform-density regimes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the interplay between band topology and strong correlations by investigating a solvable model (SSH-HK) that allows exact analytical treatment of dynamical response functions, and to assess whether standard dynamical probes can distinguish topological phases in correlated systems.

Method: Derivation of closed-form expressions for dynamical susceptibilities in the SSH-HK model using exact solvability; comparison with SSH-Hubbard and single-band Hubbard models treated via random-phase approximation; computation of quantum Fisher information as frequency-integrated imaginary part of susceptibility.

Result: The SSH-HK model exhibits finite response at zero wave vector and spectral weight restructuring across integer and fractional fillings. Quantum Fisher information shows distinct piecewise behavior distinguishing integer from fractional fillings but remains insensitive to topological phase transitions within uniform-density regimes.

Conclusion: The SSH-HK model is a powerful analytical platform for studying topology-correlation competition; dynamical susceptibilities and quantum Fisher information offer complementary, experimentally accessible probes of many-body physics, though standard response functions have limitations in characterizing band topology.

Abstract: We investigate the dynamical spin and charge susceptibilities and the associated quantum Fisher information in a class of interacting lattice models, with a primary focus on the Su-Schrieffer-Heeger model in the presence of Hatsugai-Kohmoto interactions. To provide a rigorous analytical benchmark, we contrast the response properties of the SSH-HK system with those of the single-band Hubbard and SSH-Hubbard models, treated within the random-phase approximation. While standard Hubbard-type interactions typically suppress excitation strength, we demonstrate that the SSH-HK model displays qualitatively distinct physical behavior arising from the interplay between SSH dimerization and the momentum-diagonal nature of the HK interaction. Leveraging the exact solvability of the HK term, we derive closed-form expressions for the dynamical susceptibility, revealing unique filling-controlled characteristics such as a finite response at zero wave vector and a pronounced restructuring of spectral weight across integer and fractional filling sectors. We show that the quantum Fisher information, defined as the frequency integral of the imaginary part of the susceptibility, serves as an efficient probe of these filling sectors, exhibiting distinct piecewise behavior that distinguishes integer from fractional fillings. Notably, our results indicate that the quantum Fisher information remains insensitive to topological transitions within uniform-density regimes, highlighting the limitations of standard dynamical response functions in characterizing band topology. These findings establish the SSH-HK model as a powerful analytical platform for exploring the competition between topology and strong correlations, demonstrating how dynamical susceptibilities and the quantum Fisher information provide complementary, experimentally accessible probes of many-body physics.

</details>


### [101] [Non-local low energy neutral excitations in a strongly disordered triangular Mott magnet Cr$_3$Se$_2$Br$_5$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.25269)
*Wenhao Liu,Dechen Zhang,Yuanqi Lyu,Lebing Chen,Lifang Hu,Keith M. Teddei,Yuting Zhang,Steve Shelton,Moon Kim,Xiqu Wang,Michael A. Susner,James G. Analytis,Dung-Hai Lee,Lu Li,Bing Lv,Robert J. Birgeneau*

Main category: cond-mat.str-el

TL;DR: This paper reports the discovery of charge-neutral itinerant excitations in the strongly disordered frustrated quantum magnet Cr₃Se₂Br₅, evidenced by linear temperature dependence of magnetic specific heat and thermal conductivity with a sizeable field-independent residual term, demonstrating that low-energy excitations can remain itinerant despite strong intrinsic disorder.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand whether low-energy excitations can remain itinerant in the presence of strong disorder in frustrated quantum magnets, where disorder is generally expected to localize excitations through Anderson-like mechanisms.

Method: Structural analysis, magnetic specific heat (C_mag/T) measurements, and thermal conductivity (κ_xx/T) measurements on the van der Waals compound Cr₃Se₂Br₅, a strongly disordered S=3/2 Mott insulator with a frustrated triangular lattice.

Result: Both C_mag/T and κ_xx/T exhibit linear temperature dependencies with substantial finite intercepts. A sizeable field-independent residual term κ/T ≈ 0.03 W m⁻¹ K⁻² is observed, providing evidence of itinerant low-energy excitations carrying entropy without charge.

Conclusion: The interplay of disorder, frustration, and electronic correlations can reshape low-energy excitations, allowing itinerant neutral excitations to coexist with strong intrinsic disorder, advancing the understanding of quantum matter.

Abstract: Understanding if low-energy excitations can remain itinerant in the presence of strong disorder remains a central challenge in frustrated quantum magnets, where disorder is generally expected to localize excitations through Anderson-like mechanisms. Here we report the emergence of charge-neutral itinerant excitations in a van der Waals compound Cr$_3$Se$_2$Br$_5$, a strongly disordered $S = 3/2$ Mott insulator with a frustrated triangular lattice. Structural analysis reveals substantial intrinsic disorder arising from Cr-site deficiency and Se/Br-site mixing, which appear to be fixed and cannot be readily tuned. No long-range magnetic order or conventional glassy behavior is observed. In addition to its highly insulating nature, the magnetic specific heat C_mag/T and thermal conductivity \k{appa}_xx/T both exhibit linear temperature dependencies with substantial finite intercepts. In particular, a sizeable field-independent residual term $κ/T \approx 0.03~\mathrm{W\,m^{-1}\,K^{-2}}$ is observed, providing compelling evidence of itinerant low-energy excitations that carry entropy without charge. These findings conceptually advance our understanding of quantum matter by demonstrating a rare regime where the interplay of disorder, frustration, and electronic correlations actively reshapes the nature of low-energy excitations, allowing itinerant neutral excitations to coexist with strong intrinsic disorder.

</details>


### [102] [Magnetoelastic effects in the metallic frustrated antiferromagnet CrB$_2$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.25564)
*Tadataka Watanabe,Mai Watanabe,Sakurako Suganuma,Andreas Bauer,Christian Pfleiderer*

Main category: cond-mat.str-el

TL;DR: Ultrasound measurements on CrB₂ reveal that transverse magnetoelastic coupling relieves magnetic frustration via precursor softening, while longitudinal coupling suppresses Fermi-surface nesting and enhances frustration.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine the elastic properties of hexagonal CrB₂ and understand how magnetoelastic coupling influences its frustrated antiferromagnetic state and metallic conduction.

Method: Ultrasound velocity measurements on a single crystal of hexagonal CrB₂ to determine elastic moduli (shear and compressive) as functions of temperature.

Result: The ab-plane shear modulus shows Curie-type softening from ~120 K down to TN (~88 K), indicating a precursor to symmetry-lowering lattice distortion. The a-axis and c-axis compressive moduli exhibit unusual softness and suppression upon cooling, attributed to Fermi-surface nesting and its suppression.

Conclusion: In CrB₂, longitudinal magnetoelastic coupling suppresses Fermi-surface nesting and enhances frustrated exchange interactions, while transverse magnetoelastic coupling relieves magnetic frustration.

Abstract: Hexagonal chromium diboride CrB$_2$ is a metallic frustrated antiferromagnet with a Néel temperature $T_N \sim$ 88 K. In CrB$_2$, Cr 3$d$ electrons not only give rise to localized magnetic moments but also contribute to metallic conduction. We perform ultrasound velocity measurements on a single crystal of hexagonal CrB$_2$ to determine its elastic properties. The temperature dependence of the $ab$-plane shear elastic modulus exhibits Curie-type softening upon cooling from $\sim$120 K down to $T_N$. This behavior is interpreted as a precursor to a symmetry-lowering lattice distortion at $T_N$, indicating that magnetic frustration is relieved via transverse magnetoelastic coupling. In addition, the $a$-axis and $c$-axis compressive elastic moduli show unusual softness and their suppression upon cooling, which are naturally explained by Fermi-surface nesting and its suppression upon cooling. The present results suggest that, in CrB$_2$, longitudinal magnetoelastic coupling suppresses Fermi-surface nesting and enhances frustrated exchange interactions, while transverse magnetoelastic coupling plays a key role in relieving the frustration.

</details>


### [103] [On the Relation Between String Order Parameters, Entanglement, and Dynamical Quantum Phase Transitions in Topological Dynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.25611)
*Sirshendu Bhattacharyya,Szczepan Głodzik,Nicholas Sedlmayr*

Main category: cond-mat.str-el

TL;DR: This paper investigates dynamical string order parameters and entanglement spectra following quenches in 1D topological models with Z invariants, finding no general quantitative or qualitative connection between their zeroes/crossings and dynamical quantum phase transitions, though critical times tend to be of the same order.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test whether string order parameters and entanglement spectra can serve as reliable probes of dynamical quantum phase transitions (DQPTs) out-of-equilibrium in topological models, given previous suggestions of such connections.

Method: Analytical and numerical study of quench dynamics in one-dimensional topological models characterized by Z topological invariants, examining the evolution of string order parameters, entanglement spectra, and comparing their critical behavior (zeroes and crossings) to DQPT critical times.

Result: No general quantitative or qualitative connection exists between string order parameter zeroes and DQPT critical times; entanglement spectrum crossings sometimes correlate but not typically; string order parameters can exhibit nontrivial dynamics even in quenches between unrelated topological phases; critical times are of similar order of magnitude.

Conclusion: The findings cast doubt on the existence of genuine dynamical phases following quenches in such topological models; connections found in prior work likely arose from insufficiently general model considerations.

Abstract: Topological order is defined by topological invariants, rather than symmetries and local order parameters. Nonetheless some topological phases can be characterized by string order parameters and entanglement. In this article we study how string order parameters and entanglement spectra behave out-of-equilibrium following quenches in one dimensional topological models with $\mathbb{Z}$ invariants. Previously it has been suggested that string order parameters could serve as an experimental probe of dynamical quantum phase transitions. Despite the existence of clear zeroes in the order parameters at critical times, we show that in general there is no exact quantitative or qualitative connection with the critical times of dynamical quantum phase transitions. Another possible connection is of dynamical string order parameter zeroes and dynamical crossings at the center of entanglement spectra. Here we see that there can sometimes be a connection, but it is not typical. Again there is no general quantitative or qualitative connection. Each dynamical form of criticality behaves independently, though we do see that critical times tend to be of the same order of magnitude and give an argument for why this is the case. We also find that a string order parameter which labels one topological phase can undergo non-trivial dynamics even following a quench between \emph{other} topological phases. We elucidate where connections can be made, and where they result from a consideration of insufficiently general models. These results cast doubt on the idea of genuine dynamical phases following quenches in such models.

</details>


### [104] [The evolution of pairing correlation with $3d_{z^{2}}$ electron filling in a bilayer two-orbital model for La$_3$Ni$_2$O$_7$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.25654)
*Y. F. Chen,Y. Shen,X. J. Qian,G. M. Zhang,M. P. Qin*

Main category: cond-mat.str-el

TL;DR: The paper investigates the role of the Ni 3dz² orbital in pressurized bilayer nickelate La₃Ni₂O₇ using DMRG on a 1D bilayer two-orbital model. By systematically varying the 3dz² orbital filling, they find that its itinerancy (away from half-filling) enhances superconducting correlations, while charge fluctuations compete with pairing.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the unresolved role of the Ni 3dz² orbital's itinerancy in high-Tc superconductivity of pressurized bilayer nickelate La₃Ni₂O₇, given conflicting experimental evidence on whether its Fermi pocket supports or suppresses superconductivity.

Method: Density-matrix renormalization group (DMRG) simulations on a minimal one-dimensional geometry of an effective bilayer two-orbital model for La₃Ni₂O₇, systematically varying the 3dz² orbital filling from 1/12 doping to half-filling.

Result: Superconducting correlations are strongly suppressed near 3dz² half-filling and enhanced away from it, demonstrating that itinerancy of the 3dz² orbital favors pairing. Pairing correlation is enhanced in regions with large charge fluctuations, indicating competition between charge order and superconductivity.

Conclusion: The itinerancy of the Ni 3dz² orbital is favorable for superconductivity in La₃Ni₂O₇, and charge fluctuations compete with pairing, providing key insights into the pairing mechanism of this novel high-Tc system.

Abstract: The discovery of high-${T_c}$ superconductivity in pressurized bilayer nickelate La$_3$Ni$_2$O$_7$ presents a new arena for exploring unconventional pairing mechanisms. A pivotal yet unresolved issue is the specific role of the $3d_{z^{2}}$ orbital of Ni. While its inter-layer super-exchange antiferromagnetic coupling is widely considered crucial for superconductivity, the role of its itinerancy remains undetermined. Early studies showed that the superconductivity is accompanied by the emergence of a small Fermi pocket of the $3d_{z^{2}}$ orbitals. However, recent experiments show controversial results on the role of the $3d_{z^{2}}$ Fermi pocket on superconductivity. Motivated by these experimental results, we investigate an effective bilayer two-orbital model for La$_3$Ni$_2$O$_7$ using density-matrix renormalization group (DMRG) on a minimal one-dimensional geometry. By systematically varying the $3d_{z^{2}}$ orbital filling from $1/12$ doping to half-filling, we observe a pronounced suppression of superconducting correlations near half-filling. Our results demonstrate the itinerancy of $3d_{z^{2}}$ orbital is favorable for the pairing in the bilayer two-orbital model for La$_3$Ni$_2$O$_7$. Moreover, we observe that the pairing correlation is enhanced in regions where charge fluctuations are large, suggesting a competition between charge order and superconductivity in the model.

</details>


### [105] [Near-Room-Temperature Antiferromagnetic Ordering in the Quadruple Perovskite Sr4NaRu3O12](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.25694)
*Subham Naik,Biswajit Singh,Hiranmayee Senapati,Akshay K. U.,Ramesh C. Nath,Soumyojit Chatterjee,Rahul Sharma,Thomas Doert,Walter Schnelle,Manfred Reehuis,Thomas C. Hansen,Michael Ruck,Gohil S. Thakur*

Main category: cond-mat.str-el

TL;DR: This paper reports the synthesis and characterization of two 1:3 ordered quadruple perovskites Sr4MRu3O12 (M = Li, Na). Sr4NaRu3O12 adopts a centrosymmetric R-3 structure with ordered Na/Ru at B sites, exhibits antiferromagnetic ordering below ~265 K with collinear spin alignment along the c-axis and a propagation vector k = (0,0,1.5), and shows semiconducting behavior. Ru moments at three-fold roto-inversion sites are highly frustrated. Sr4LiRu3O12 is isostructural but shows a magnetic anomaly below 110 K with competing ferromagnetic and antiferromagnetic interactions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To synthesize and investigate the structure, magnetic properties, and electronic behavior of 1:3 ordered quadruple perovskites Sr4MRu3O12 (M = Li, Na), exploring the effects of B-site ordering on magnetic frustration and ground states.

Method: Synthesis via solid-state reactions; structural characterization by PXRD and neutron diffraction; magnetic measurements (susceptibility); DSC; band structure calculations.

Result: Sr4NaRu3O12: centrosymmetric R-3 structure, large unit cell (a=11.25Å, c=27.6Å), antiferromagnetic transition at TN~265 K, collinear spin alignment along c-axis with k=(0,0,1.5), frustrated Ru moments at roto-inversion sites, semiconducting behavior. Sr4LiRu3O12: isostructural to Na compound, magnetic anomaly below 110 K with competing FM/AFM interactions.

Conclusion: The 1:3 B-site ordering in these quadruple perovskites leads to a complex magnetic landscape with frustration and distinct antiferromagnetic ordering, with the Na compound showing a clear AFM ground state while the Li compound exhibits competing interactions.

Abstract: We report the synthesis, structure and magnetic properties of two 1:3 ordered quadruple perovskites Sr4MRu3O12 (M = Li and Na). Sr4NaRu3O12 crystallizes in the centrosymmetric space group R-3 and Sr4LiRu3O12 appears to be isostructural to the Na compound based on the PXRD data. In Sr4NaRu3O12, both Na and Ru are predominantly ordered at the B sites (here Na/Li and Ru) and the structure contains only corner-connected RuO6 and NaO6 octahedra. This atomic ordering also leads to a rather large unit cell with a = 11.25 Å and c = 27.6 Å compared to the basic 12R structure (a = 5.5 Å and c ~ 27 Å). Magnetic measurements reveal that Sr4NaRu3O12 undergoes a magnetic transition to an antiferromagnetic state below TN ~ 265 K which is confirmed by DSC and neutron diffraction. The Ru moments show a collinear antiferromagnetic spin alignment along the hexagonal c axis with a propagation vector k = (0, 0, 1.5). Interestingly, those Ru moments lying on the three-fold roto-inversion do not significantly contribute to the magnetic order, since they are located between antiferromagnetically coupled Ru atoms and are therefore probably highly frustrated. Band structure calculations on Sr4NaRu3O12 complement the observed magnetic ground state and a semiconducting behavior in the compound. Sr4LiRu3O12 shows a magnetic anomaly below 110 K, possibly associated with competing ferromagnetic and antiferromagnetic interactions.

</details>


### [106] [Trimerized Spin-$1/2$ Chain: Emergent Low-Energy Hamiltonian, Higher-Energy Excitations, and Magnetic and Thermodynamic Responses](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.25960)
*Snehasish Sen,Sudhansu S. Mandal*

Main category: cond-mat.str-el

TL;DR: A study of a spin-1/2 antiferromagnetic chain with trimerized exchange couplings (relevant to Na₂Cu₃Ge₄O₁₂) showing that the low-energy Hilbert space maps onto an effective Heisenberg chain of composite spin-1/2 trimers, enabling exact spinon solutions via Bethe ansatz. A self-consistent Jordan-Wigner mean-field theory is developed to access higher-energy dynamics, revealing three fermionic bands, reproducing the exact low-energy spinon continuum, and predicting two additional higher-energy excitation bands consistent with experiments and numerics. The theory also captures the 1/3 magnetization plateau and yields testable predictions for magnetic susceptibility and specific heat.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the interplay between low- and high-energy excitations in trimerized quantum spin chains, particularly for the material Na₂Cu₃Ge₄O₁₂, and to develop a unified theoretical framework that connects exact low-energy solutions to higher-energy dynamics.

Method: Mapping the low-energy Hilbert space onto an effective Heisenberg chain of composite spin-1/2 trimers with positive coupling, solved exactly via Bethe ansatz for spinon excitations. A self-consistent Jordan-Wigner mean-field theory is developed to access higher-energy dynamics, yielding three fermionic bands that reflect the trimer structure.

Result: The Jordan-Wigner mean-field approach reproduces the exact low-energy spinon continuum and predicts two additional higher-energy excitation bands consistent with experiments and previous numerical simulations. The theory also captures the 1/3 magnetization plateau under an applied magnetic field.

Conclusion: A unified framework connecting low- and high-energy excitations in trimerized quantum spin chains is established, with testable predictions in magnetic susceptibility and specific heat for Na₂Cu₃Ge₄O₁₂.

Abstract: We investigate a spin-1/2 antiferromagnetic chain with trimerized exchange couplings relevant to Na$_2$Cu$_3$Ge$_4$O$_{12}$. We show that the low-energy Hilbert space maps onto an effective Heisenberg chain of composite spin-1/2 trimer degrees of freedom with positive coupling, enabling an exact spinon excitations via the Bethe ansatz. To access higher-energy dynamics, we develop a self-consistent Jordan-Wigner mean-field theory that yields three fermionic bands reflecting the underlying trimer structure. Remarkably, this approach reproduces the exact low-energy spinon continuum while predicting two additional higher-energy excitation bands consistent with the experimental observations and previous numerical simulations. The theory further captures the 1/3 magnetization plateau under an applied magnetic field, and provides testable predictions in magnetic susceptibility and specific heat. Our results establish a unified framework connecting low- and high-energy excitations in trimerized quantum spin chains.

</details>


### [107] [Demagnetization effect on magnetic noise measurements in spin ice materials](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.25993)
*F. Morineau,C. Paulsen,G. Balakrishnan,D. Prabhakaran,K. Matsuhira,S. R. Giblin,E. Lhotel*

Main category: cond-mat.str-el

TL;DR: Magnetic noise spectra in spin ice are strongly influenced by sample geometry (demagnetizing fields), not just intrinsic fluctuations, so shape must be treated as a control parameter and demagnetization corrections cannot be derived from noise data alone.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how demagnetizing fields affect magnetic noise spectroscopy in correlated magnetic systems like spin ice, and to determine whether noise spectra directly reflect intrinsic fluctuation dynamics.

Method: Combined magnetic noise and ac susceptibility measurements on single-crystal spin ice samples of varying sizes and shapes.

Result: Magnetic noise is strongly renormalized by sample geometry; measured spectra do not directly yield intrinsic fluctuation spectra; demagnetization corrections cannot be determined from noise data alone.

Conclusion: Sample geometry must be treated as a central experimental control parameter for accessing intrinsic dynamics; boundary conditions play a key role in fluctuation spectroscopy, and a framework is established for quantitative comparison with microscopic theories.

Abstract: Magnetic noise spectroscopy provides direct access to spontaneous time-dependent magnetization fluctuations in correlated magnetic systems, including spin liquids, spin ices, and spin glasses. Here we investigate how demagnetizing fields shape the magnetic noise spectra measured in spin ice. By combining magnetic noise and ac susceptibility measurements on single-crystal spin ice samples with different sizes and shapes, we show that magnetic noise, like linear response, is strongly renormalized by sample geometry. As a consequence, measured noise spectra do not directly yield the intrinsic fluctuation spectrum, and demagnetization corrections cannot in general be determined from noise data alone. Instead, sample geometry must be treated as a central experimental control parameter for accessing intrinsic dynamics. These results establish the role of boundary conditions in fluctuation spectroscopy and provide a framework for quantitatively comparing noise measurements with microscopic theories of spin dynamics.

</details>


### [108] [Magnetic ground state of a prototype quasicrystal approximant: a candidate for octahedral spin ice physics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.26044)
*Leonie Woodland,Farid Labib,Dmitry Khalyavin,Pascal Manuel,Fabio Orlandi,Hubertus Luetkens,Ryuji Tamura*

Main category: cond-mat.str-el

TL;DR: The paper investigates magnetic ordering in the Tsai-type quasicrystal approximant Cd6Tb, revealing a noncoplanar multi-k magnetic ground state with coexisting ordered and fluctuating Tb moments, which represents a potential realization of magnetic fragmentation in a quasicrystal-related material.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To elucidate the microscopic origin of unconventional magnetic states in quasicrystals, which remains challenging due to intrinsic aperiodicity.

Method: Neutron diffraction measurements and muon spin rotation spectroscopy on the Tsai-type quasicrystal approximant Cd6Tb.

Result: A noncoplanar multi-k magnetic ground state of Ising-like Tb moments on corner-sharing octahedra; only one third of moments develop long-range order while the rest show reduced static order with persistent spin dynamics on microsecond timescales.

Conclusion: The observed coexistence of ordered and fluctuating moments constitutes a potential realization of magnetic fragmentation, a key prediction of octahedral spin-ice physics, in a quasicrystal-related material.

Abstract: Magnetic ordering in quasicrystals has recently emerged as a fertile ground for discovering unconventional magnetic states beyond the framework of periodic crystals. However, elucidating the microscopic origin of such states remains challenging due to the intrinsic aperiodicity of quasicrystals. Here, we address this issue by investigating the prototypical Tsai-type quasicrystal approximant Cd6Tb, which preserves the essential local geometry and connectivity of icosahedral quasicrystals while allowing detailed structural and magnetic characterization due to its translational periodicity. Using neutron diffraction measurements, we find a noncoplanar multi-k magnetic ground state composed of Ising-like Tb moments arranged on a network of corner-sharing octahedra, the ingredients required to host octahedral spin-ice physics. Remarkably, only one third of the Tb moments develop long-range magnetic order, whereas the remaining moments display strongly reduced static order accompanied by persistent spin dynamics on microsecond timescales, as evidenced by muon spin rotation. This coexistence of ordered and fluctuating moments constitutes a potential realization of magnetic fragmentation - a key prediction of octahedral spin-ice physics - in a quasicrystal-related material.

</details>


<div id='cond-mat.dis-nn'></div>

# cond-mat.dis-nn [[Back]](#toc)

### [109] [Anderson Localization: A Floquet operator Krylov space perspective](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.24115)
*Hsiu-Chung Yeh,Aditi Mitra*

Main category: cond-mat.dis-nn

TL;DR: This paper studies Anderson localization and the Aubry-André model using operator Krylov space methods, showing advantages of stroboscopic over continuous-time dynamics, mapping to an effective Floquet transverse field Ising model, and identifying localization-delocalization transitions via Porter-Thomas distributions, ballistic vs. localized wavefronts, and Bernstein-Szegö spectra.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To study Anderson localization and the single-particle localization-delocalization transition of the Aubry-André model using operator Krylov space methods, leveraging stroboscopic dynamics for computational advantages.

Method: Operator Krylov space methods applied to stroboscopic (Floquet) dynamics; mapping to an inhomogeneous Floquet transverse field Ising model; recursive generation of Krylov parameters; analysis of disorder-averaged autocorrelation functions, Porter-Thomas distributions, wavefront propagation, and Bernstein-Szegö power spectra.

Result: Stroboscopic dynamics reduces computational cost; Krylov parameters show disorder-averaged renormalization with narrowing distributions; physical spectral functions are better obtained from disorder-averaged autocorrelation functions; delocalized phase exhibits Porter-Thomas distribution, ballistic wavefront, and smooth Bernstein-Szegö spectrum; localized phase shows absence of Porter-Thomas, localized wavefront, and discrete spectrum; critical point shows Porter-Thomas distribution and multi-fractal scaling.

Conclusion: Operator Krylov space methods with stroboscopic dynamics effectively capture the localization-delocalization transition, and the critical point exhibits multifractal behavior consistent with previous studies.

Abstract: The problem of Anderson localization, as well as the single particle localization-delocalizaton transition of the Aubry-André model, is studied employing operator Krylov space methods. It is shown that even when the dynamics is generated by a Hamiltonian, studying the dynamics at stroboscopic rather than continuous times has its advantages. In particular, mapping the dynamics to an effective Floquet problem results in an operator Krylov space description where quantities such as the spectral function can be computed with fewer computational resources, while a moment method exists that allows for the extraction of Krylov parameters directly from the discrete time autocorrelation function. For stroboscopic dynamics, the operator Krylov space corresponds to the dynamics of an edge operator of an inhomogeneous Floquet transverse field Ising model, with the parameters of this effective model generated recursively. The Krylov parameters show disorder-averaged renormalization with their distribution narrowing as the recursion step increases. It is shown that a more physical spectral function is obtained from the Krylov parameters obtained from the disorder-averaged autocorrelation function, rather than the disorder-averaged Krylov parameters. The delocalized (localized) phase is shown to correspond to the appearance (absence) of a Porter-Thomas distribution, a ballistically propagating (localized) wavefront in operator Krylov space, and a smooth (discrete) Berstein-Szegö power-spectrum. The localization-delocalization transition is also demonstrated in operator Krylov space. A Porter-Thomas distribution is also observed at the critical point. The long-time dynamics and the inverse participation ratio at the critical point is shown to exhibit behavior consistent with a multi-fractal scaling with system size.

</details>


### [110] [Lateral Shift as a Control Knob for Localization Transitions in a Quasiperiodic Ladder](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.25033)
*Bing Shao,Guangjie Zhang,Longwen Zhou,Jiangbin Gong,Weiwei Zhu*

Main category: cond-mat.dis-nn

TL;DR: This paper studies localization-delocalization transitions in a quasiperiodic ladder with lateral shifts between legs, which generate effective magnetic flux, enabling control over localization phenomena including enhanced, suppressed, and reentrant transitions, explained via band-structure analysis.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate how lateral shifts in a quasiperiodic ladder generate effective magnetic flux and control localization-delocalization transitions, providing a tunable platform for exploring localization physics.

Method: Band-structure analysis using a commensurate approximation to the quasiperiodic potential, requiring only unit cells of small to modest sizes, to understand phase boundaries and underlying mechanisms.

Result: Rich localization-delocalization transitions observed: magnetic-flux-enhanced localization, magnetic-flux-suppressed localization, and magnetic-flux-induced reentrant localization transitions, with phase boundaries identified for localized, mixed, and extended phases.

Conclusion: The work provides a highly tunable platform for localization physics with applications such as quantum switching, and a broadly applicable approach for understanding localization-delocalization transitions in quasiperiodic systems.

Abstract: This work reports rich localization-delocalization transitions in a quasiperiodic ladder, of which the two legs are subject to the same quasiperiodic onsite potential but can be shifted laterally relative to each other. It is found that the lateral shift between the two legs effectively generates a magnetic flux in the reciprocal momentum space. The lateral shift thus offers a control knob, allowing us to access and simulate rich phenomena including magnetic-flux-enhanced localization, magnetic-flux-suppressed localization, and magnetic-flux-induced reentrant localization transitions. The underlying physical mechanisms as well as the phase boundaries separating localized, mixed, and extended phases are both qualitatively and quantitatively understood, based on a band-structure analysis that employs a commensurate approximation to the quasiperiodic potential, requiring only unit cells of small to modest sizes. Our work provides a highly tunable platform for exploring localization physics with promising applications such as quantum switching, and a broadly applicable approach for understanding localization-delocalization transitions in quasiperiodic systems.

</details>


### [111] [Topological metal-insulator transitions in one-dimensional non-Hermitian quasicrystals: beyond PT-symmetry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.25072)
*Guangjie Zhang,Bing Shao,Longwen Zhou,Jiangbin Gong,Weiwei Zhu*

Main category: cond-mat.dis-nn

TL;DR: This paper investigates how the absence of PT symmetry impacts topological metal-insulator transitions in non-Hermitian quasicrystals, proposing a non-PT-symmetric quasiperiodic model that exhibits triple phase transitions (localization, topology, degeneracy-breaking) in most parameter regions, and revealing distinct types of localization behavior beyond PT-symmetric systems.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how the absence of PT symmetry influences topological metal-insulator transitions in non-Hermitian quasicrystals, extending previous studies that focused on PT-symmetric systems.

Method: Proposing a non-Hermitian quasiperiodic model that generally does not preserve PT symmetry, and analyzing its phase transitions across parameter regimes.

Result: In most parameter regions, the system supports triple phase transitions encompassing localization, topology, and degeneracy-breaking. The system can also exhibit a localization-delocalization transition analogous to the Hermitian case without activating topological or degeneracy-breaking transitions.

Conclusion: The work extends topological metal-insulator transitions from PT-symmetric to more general non-Hermitian settings, revealing that non-Hermitian systems can host distinct types of localization behavior.

Abstract: One-dimensional non-Hermitian quasicrystals with parity and time-reversal (PT) symmetry can simultaneously exhibit localization-delocalization transition, topological phase transition, and PT-symmetry-breaking transition. This motivates this work to investigate how the absence of PT symmetry impacts topological metal-insulator transitions in non-Hermitian quasicrystals. We propose a non-Hermitian quasiperiodic model that generally does not preserve PT symmetry and demonstrate that, in most parameter regions, such a system supports triple phase transitions that encompass localization, topology, and degeneracy-breaking. The system may also exhibit a particular type of localization-delocalization transition analogous to the Hermitian case, namely, without activating topological phase transitions or degeneracy-breaking transitions. Our work extends the topological metal-insulator transitions previously studied in PT-symmetric systems to a more general class of non-Hermitian setting, and further reveals that non-Hermitian systems can host distinct types of localization behavior.

</details>


### [112] [Sensitivity to perturbations in the three-dimensional Anderson model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.25594)
*Piotr Tokarczyk,Lev Vidmar,Anatoli Polkovnikov,Patrycja Łydżba*

Main category: cond-mat.dis-nn

TL;DR: The study examines fidelity susceptibility in the 3D Anderson model, finding two peaks: one at weak disorder signaling a crossover to quantum chaos (scaling as square of inverse frequency cutoff), and another at the Anderson localization transition (submaximal divergence due to fractal eigenstates). Evidence of two distinct nonergodic regimes is found above the transition.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how single-particle eigenstates respond to perturbations across different disorder strengths, particularly near the Anderson localization transition and in the weak-disorder regime.

Method: Numerical computation of fidelity susceptibility in the three-dimensional Anderson model, analyzing scaling behavior of peak divergences and typical fidelity susceptibilities.

Result: Two distinct peaks are identified: the first (weak disorder) corresponds to a crossover from plane waves to quantum chaos with maximal divergence scaling as the square of inverse frequency cutoff; the second (at critical disorder) marks the Anderson transition with submaximal divergence suppressed by fractal eigenstate structure. Two nonergodic regimes are observed above the transition.

Conclusion: Fidelity susceptibility serves as a sensitive probe for both the weak-disorder crossover to quantum chaos and the Anderson localization transition, with distinct scaling signatures arising from different physical mechanisms (ergodic vs. fractal critical eigenstates).

Abstract: We investigate the fidelity susceptibility, which quantifies the sensitivity of single-particle eigenstates to perturbations, in the three-dimensional Anderson model. As a function of disorder strength $W$, it exhibits two distinct peaks. The first peak signals a crossover at weak disorder strength from plane-wave states to single-particle quantum chaos, and its position shifts toward $W\to 0$ in the thermodynamic limit. The second peak emerges, to high numerical accuracy, at the critical disorder strength associated with the Anderson localization transition. We further show that the divergence of the first peak is maximal, scaling as the square of the inverse frequency cutoff, whereas the divergence of the second peak is submaximal. We relate the latter suppression to the fractal structure of single-particle eigenstates at criticality. We discuss two distinct scenarios that give rise to the peaks in the fidelity susceptibilities. Moreover, studying the scaling of typical fidelity susceptibilities above the Anderson transition, we find evidence of two distinct regimes of nonergodic behavior.

</details>


### [113] [Spectral properties of non-Hermitian real random matrices with long-range correlations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.25736)
*Ulysse Marquis*

Main category: cond-mat.dis-nn

TL;DR: This paper studies non-Hermitian random matrices with long-range correlated entries, finding a transition in spectral properties controlled by the correlation decay exponent α: for α>1 the circular law holds with an effective radius, while for α<1 the spectral radius grows as a power law reminiscent of the extended central limit theorem, with α=1 as a critical case.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how long-range correlations in matrix entries affect the eigenvalue statistics of non-Hermitian random matrices, beyond the standard independent-entry circular law.

Method: Numerical spectral analysis of non-Hermitian real random matrices with power-law correlated entries decaying as |r−r'|^{-α}, varying the correlation exponent α across the critical value α=1.

Result: A correlation-driven transition at α=1: for α>1 the circular law spectrum is retrieved; for α<1 the spectral radius grows with matrix size following power laws close to extended central limit theorem exponents; α=1 shows self-similar eigenvalue density. Long-range correlations also enhance real eigenvalue clustering and slow the Saturn effect resorption.

Conclusion: The findings reveal a new universality class for correlated non-Hermitian random matrices, with a transition controlled by the correlation decay exponent.

Abstract: We investigate the spectral properties of non-Hermitian real random matrices whose entries exhibit long-range correlations decaying as~$|r-r'|^{-α}$. We find a progressive breakdown of the circular law, controlled by the decrease of~$α$. In all cases, the radial eigenvalue density decreases away from the origin. At~$α>1$, an effective radius, reminiscent of the circular law, is retrieved, while instead, for~$α<1$, the eigenvalue distribution broadens with matrix size and its spectral radius grows like a power law, with exponents numerically close to the exponents controlling the magnitude of fluctuations in the extended central limit theorem. The case~$α=1$ appears as a case with self-similar eigenvalue density, and slowly growing spectral radius. Long-range correlations also enhance clustering of real eigenvalues and slow the resorption of the Saturn effect. These results reveal a correlation-driven transition and suggest the emergence of a new universality class for correlated non-Hermitian random matrices.

</details>


### [114] [Cluster moves with an entropic reservoir accelerate low-temperature simulations of three-dimensional spin glasses](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.25872)
*Claudio Chilin,Enzo Marinari,Víctor Martín-Mayor,Giorgio Parisi,Juan J. Ruiz-Lorenzo,David Yllanes*

Main category: cond-mat.dis-nn

TL;DR: PTHR algorithm enhances Parallel Tempering with Houdayer moves and entropic reservoir for better equilibration of 3D spin glasses at low temperatures, achieving ~64x speedup over other cluster methods and showing improved size scaling.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To efficiently simulate three-dimensional spin glasses deep in the low-temperature phase, where standard algorithms struggle with equilibration and computational complexity.

Method: Parallel Tempering enhanced with Houdayer moves and an entropic reservoir (PTHR), applied to L=16 lattices with Gaussian couplings at temperatures T≥0.2.

Result: PTHR equilibrates many samples successfully, shows better size scaling than standard Parallel Tempering, and achieves ~64x speedup over other cluster algorithms for finite sizes.

Conclusion: PTHR outperforms existing methods for low-temperature spin glass simulation, and its computational complexity is strongly linked to temperature chaos, similar to standard Parallel Tempering.

Abstract: We present an algorithm for the simulation of three-dimensional spin glasses deep in the low-temperature phase: Parallel Tempering enhanced with Houdayer moves and with an entropic reservoir (PTHR). Although differences with the standard Houdayer algorithm are small, PTHR allows us to equilibrate a large number of samples of $L=16$ lattices with Gaussian couplings for temperatures $T\geq 0.2$. We show that the computational complexity displays better size scaling than standard Parallel Tempering. For finite sizes, our method outperforms other cluster algorithms by a speedup factor of around 64. In close analogy with standard Parallel Tempering, PTHR's computational complexity strongly relates to temperature chaos.

</details>


<div id='cond-mat.supr-con'></div>

# cond-mat.supr-con [[Back]](#toc)

### [115] [A helical Rashba--exchange gauge field drives a uniaxial pair density wave in EuRbFe$_4$As$_4$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.24362)
*Pengfei Li,Yi Zhou*

Main category: cond-mat.supr-con

TL;DR: The interplay of Rashba spin-orbit coupling and helical magnetic order in EuRbFe$_4$As$_4$ generates a gauge field that induces an orbital-selective, uniaxial pair density wave (PDW) accompanied by spontaneous interlayer loop currents.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the recent discovery of an intrinsic pair density wave (PDW) in the iron-pnictide superconductor EuRbFe$_4$As$_4$, which presents a puzzle due to its broken local inversion symmetry and helical magnetic order.

Method: Using a phenomenological Ginzburg-Landau theory, the authors model the interplay of induced Rashba spin-orbit coupling and the helical exchange field, showing it generates a layer-rotating effective U(1) gauge field that couples to Fe 3d_{xz}/3d_{yz} orbitals.

Result: The mechanism naturally stabilizes a strictly uniaxial (unidirectional) Bloch-type PDW state, capturing STM phenomenology, and predicts spontaneous loop currents between adjacent layers as a distinctive signature.

Conclusion: The proposed orbital-selective pairing mechanism explains the observed PDW in EuRbFe$_4$As$_4$ and predicts interlayer loop currents detectable by μSR or scanning SQUID experiments.

Abstract: The recent discovery of an intrinsic pair density wave (PDW) in the iron-pnictide superconductor EuRbFe$_4$As$_4$ presents a fascinating puzzle. Structurally, the FeAs layers break local inversion symmetry, while the Eu$^{2+}$ spins exhibit a period-four helical magnetic order. Here, we demonstrate that the interplay of induced Rashba spin-orbit coupling and the helical exchange field generates a layer-rotating effective $U(1)$ gauge field for the Cooper pairs. Because this gauge field shares the symmetry of the Fe $3d_{xz}/3d_{yz}$ orbitals, it explicitly induces an intertwined, orbital-selective pairing state. Using a phenomenological Ginzburg-Landau theory, we show this mechanism naturally stabilizes a structurally modulated Bloch superconducting state. This state features a strictly uniaxial (unidirectional) PDW, naturally capturing the phenomenology of recent scanning tunneling microscopy experiments. Furthermore, we predict this PDW is accompanied by spontaneous loop currents between adjacent layers, offering a distinctive signature for future $μ$SR or scanning SQUID experiments.

</details>


### [116] [Emergence of Triplet Superconductivity from Cavity Vacuum Fluctuations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.24439)
*Xin-Xin Yang,Shuai Zhang,Kun Ding,Xiaopeng Li*

Main category: cond-mat.supr-con

TL;DR: Cavity vacuum fluctuations can drive a transition from singlet to triplet superconductivity by renormalizing the electronic band structure and reshaping the Fermi surface, enabling unconventional superconducting phases absent in the bare material.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To demonstrate that cavity vacuum fluctuations alone can induce triplet superconductivity in an otherwise singlet superconductor, providing a mechanism for generating unconventional superconducting phases without external driving.

Method: Theoretical analysis of cavity quantum electrodynamics effects on superconductivity, where vacuum fluctuations renormalize the electronic band structure in a polarization-dependent manner, reshaping the Fermi surface and altering the competition among symmetry-allowed pairing channels.

Result: Above a critical light-matter coupling, the leading pairing instability switches from singlet to triplet, producing multiple superconducting phases with distinct gap structures and low-energy quasiparticle spectra.

Conclusion: Cavity vacuum engineering offers a mechanism for generating unconventional superconducting phases and stabilizing triplet states, with potential relevance for topological superconductivity.

Abstract: Engineering quantum materials with cavity fields has emerged as a powerful route to manipulate phases of quantum matter in solids. Here we demonstrate that cavity vacuum fluctuations alone can drive the emergence of triplet superconductivity in an otherwise singlet superconductor. The vacuum field renormalizes the electronic band structure in a polarization dependent manner, reshaping the Fermi surface and altering the competition among symmetry allowed pairing channels. As a result, multiple superconducting phases arise from the cavity vacuum fluctuations. Above a critical light matter coupling, the leading instability switches from singlet to triplet pairing, yielding a superconducting state absent in the bare material. This vacuum induced symmetry transition produces distinct modifications of the gap structure and low energy quasiparticle spectrum. Our results establish cavity vacuum engineering as a mechanism for generating unconventional superconducting phases and stabilizing triplet states of potential relevance for topological superconductivity.

</details>


### [117] [Synchronization of Bloch Oscillations in array of parallel Josephson Junctions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.24522)
*R. Shaikhaidarov,R. Hussain,V. N. Antonov,E. Il'ichev*

Main category: cond-mat.supr-con

TL;DR: The paper demonstrates synchronized current quantization in a parallel array of 69 weakly coupled Josephson junctions operating in the coherent quantum phase slip regime, achieving a first quantized current step at 426 nA under 19.325 GHz microwave excitation, with accuracy that does not degrade with increasing junction count.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the quantized current amplitude requirements for a practical quantum current standard, as part of developing the missing current standard in the triangle of electrical units (alongside established Josephson voltage and Quantum Hall resistance standards).

Method: A parallel array of 69 weakly coupled Josephson junctions operating in the coherent quantum phase slip regime was used, with microwave excitation at 19.325 GHz, and measurement of voltage-current characteristics to observe quantized current steps.

Result: The first quantized current step was observed at 426 nA, and the quantization accuracy does not degrade with increasing number of combined Josephson junctions.

Conclusion: This work demonstrates scalability for practical quantum current standards, though the question of metrological accuracy (to match the established Volt and Ohm standards) requires further research.

Abstract: We demonstrate the synchronization of current quantization in a parallel array of weakly coupled Josephson Junctions operating in the regime of the coherent quantum phase slip. The first quantized current step on the voltage-current characteristic of 69 parallel Josephson junctions, under microwave excitation at a frequency of f = 19.325 GHz, is observed at 426 nA. Experiments show that accuracy of quantization does not degrade with increasing number of combined Josephson junctions. This demonstration addresses the issue of quantized current amplitude requirements adopted for a practical quantum standard, while leaving the question of accuracy for further research. At present, the quantum current standard of accepted metrological accuracy in the triangle of electrical units is under development. The other two, the Volt and Ohm standards based on the Josephson and Quantum Hall effects, respectively, are already well established in metrology.

</details>


### [118] [Nonunitary triplet superconductivity in the Z2 topological metal SrPd2As2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.24536)
*Aarti,Dibyendu Samanta,Kartik Panda,Devashibhai Adroja,Daloo Ram,Zakir Hossain,Rhea Stewart,Adrian Hillier,Amitava Bhattacharyya,Samar Layek,Sudeep Kumar Ghosh,Vivek Kumar Anand*

Main category: cond-mat.supr-con

TL;DR: SrPd2As2 is identified as a Z2 topological metal where nonunitary triplet pairing with symmetry-imposed nodes in unpopulated momentum-space regions yields a fully gapped thermodynamic response and spontaneous time-reversal symmetry breaking, establishing it as a platform for intrinsic topological superconductivity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the apparent contradiction in Z2 topological metals where thermodynamic probes indicate an isotropic gap despite nontrivial band topology and strong spin-orbit coupling that can stabilize unconventional superconducting states.

Method: Combined muon spin rotation/relaxation (muSR), first-principles electronic structure calculations, and Ginzburg-Landau analysis, along with anisotropic Migdal-Eliashberg calculations.

Result: Transverse-field muSR shows a fully gapped superconducting state (Tc=0.94 K); zero-field muSR detects spontaneous internal magnetic fields below Tc, confirming TRS breaking. Electronic structure calculations reveal Z2 topological metal with surface states. Standard phonon-mediated calculations predict a nodal gap and overestimate Tc. The interplay of SOC, tetragonal symmetry, and open Fermi surface topology stabilizes a nonunitary triplet state whose nodes lie in momentum regions devoid of electronic states.

Conclusion: SrPd2As2 provides a clean platform for bulk nonunitary triplet pairing and is a promising candidate for intrinsic topological superconductivity, resolving the gap between thermodynamic isotropy and unconventional pairing symmetry.

Abstract: In Z2 topological metals, nontrivial band topology and strong spin-orbit coupling (SOC) impose symmetry constraints that can stabilize unconventional superconducting states, even when thermodynamic probes indicate an isotropic gap. Here, we investigate the superconducting ground state of such a material, SrPd2As2, using muon spin rotation and relaxation (muSR), first-principles calculations, and Ginzburg-Landau analysis. Transverse-field muSR indicates a fully gapped superconducting state below Tc = 0.94 K, while zero-field muSR detects spontaneous internal magnetic fields below Tc, establishing time-reversal symmetry (TRS) breaking. Electronic structure calculations identify SrPd2As2 as a Z2 topological metal with surface states crossing the Fermi level. Standard anisotropic Migdal-Eliashberg calculations predict a nodal gap and overestimate Tc, indicating that a purely phonon-mediated pairing mechanism is insufficient. We resolve this apparent contradiction by showing that the interplay of SOC, tetragonal symmetry, and an open Fermi surface topology stabilizes a nonunitary triplet superconducting state whose symmetry-imposed nodes lie in momentum-space regions devoid of electronic states. This yields a fully gapped thermodynamic response while naturally breaking TRS. Our results establish SrPd2As2 as a clean platform for bulk nonunitary triplet pairing and a promising candidate for intrinsic topological superconductivity.

</details>


### [119] [Theoretical study of superconductivity in freestanding infinite-layer nickelate membranes under pressure: mitigation of excess correlation enhances $T_c$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.24565)
*Mahiru Seki,Reo Kono,Naotaka Tanaka,Kensei Ushio,Daiki Nakaoka,Masayuki Ochi,Kazuhiko Kuroki,Hirofumi Sakakibara*

Main category: cond-mat.supr-con

TL;DR: A theoretical study of infinite-layer nickelate Nd₀.₈₅Sr₀.₁₅NiO₂ under pressure shows that superconductivity (Tc) increases monotonically due to reduced electron correlations from low Ni valence, supported by FLEX and phonon calculations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the experimentally observed pressure-induced increase in Tc in infinite-layer nickelates and to identify its microscopic mechanism.

Method: Construction of a seven-orbital effective model from first-principles calculations, combined with fluctuation exchange (FLEX) approximation for superconductivity analysis and phonon calculations for structural stability.

Result: The seven-orbital model reproduces the monotonic increase in Tc under pressure; the enhancement is attributed to mitigation of excessively strong electron correlations due to the exceptionally low valence of Ni atoms; crystal structure remains dynamically stable under pressure.

Conclusion: Pressure mitigates strong correlations in low-valence Ni, enhancing superconductivity in infinite-layer nickelates without structural instability.

Abstract: We theoretically investigate a freestanding membrane of infinite-layer nickelate Nd$_{0.85}$Sr$_{0.15}$NiO$_2$ under pressure by constructing a seven-orbital effective model based on first-principles calculations.
  By performing the fluctuation exchange (FLEX) approximation, we demonstrate that the seven-orbital model explains a monotonic increase in $T_c$ reported in a recent experiment. This enhancement of superconductivity is attributed to the mitigation of excessively strong electron correlations caused by exceptionally low valence of Ni atom. Furthermore, we examine the dynamical stability of the crystal structure under pressure through phonon calculation.

</details>


### [120] [Prediction of 1:1 kagome metals with superconductivity and band topology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.24822)
*Na Jiao,Shu-Xiang Qiao,Pan Zhou,Hong-Yan Lu,Ping Zhang*

Main category: cond-mat.supr-con

TL;DR: First-principles calculations predict a new family of 1:1 kagome MSn (M=transition metal) materials that exhibit intrinsic superconductivity and nontrivial band topology, with five non-magnetic candidates showing phonon-mediated superconductivity and three showing nontrivial topological band structures at the Fermi level.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To discover a new class of kagome materials that intrinsically combine superconductivity and topological order, overcoming the limitation of existing kagome materials like FeSn and CoSn where intrinsic magnetism suppresses superconductivity.

Method: First-principles calculations (density functional theory) screening 27 MSn candidates for dynamical and thermodynamic stability, electronic structure analysis for Dirac points and van Hove singularities, and evaluation of electron-phonon coupling for superconductivity.

Result: Six MSn (M=Mo, Hf, Nb, Ta, W, Ti) are dynamically and thermodynamically stable. Five non-magnetic MSn (M=Mo, Hf, Nb, Ta, W) exhibit phonon-mediated superconductivity. MoSn, HfSn, and NbSn show nontrivial topological band structures at the Fermi level, with d-orbital bands contributing Dirac points, van Hove singularities, and electron-phonon coupling.

Conclusion: The predicted MSn kagome materials provide a platform that integrates superconductivity and topological order, enabling further study of correlated physics and topological phenomena in a single material system.

Abstract: Kagome superconductors featuring topologically nontrivial band structures have attracted extensive research interest. FeSn and CoSn is a new kind of kagome material with intrinsic magnetism, which suppresses the emergence of superconductivity. Here, we theoretically predict a new kind of 1:1 kagome MSn (M=transition metal), which exhibit intrinsic superconductivity and nontrivial band topology by first-principles calculations. Among twenty-seven candidates, MSn (M= Mo, Hf, Nb, Ta, W, Ti) are theoretically identified as both dynamically and thermodynamical stable. And, five non-magnetic MSn (M= Mo, Hf, Nb, Ta, W) exhibit phonon-mediated superconductivity. Especially, the d orbitals bands display Dirac points and van Hove singularities near the Fermi level, which contribute to the emergence of topology and the electron-phonon coupling (EPC). More interestingly, MoSn, HfSn and NbSn show nontrivial topological band structure at the Fermi level. Thus, the predicted MSn establish a platform integrating superconductivity and topological order.

</details>


### [121] [Superconductivity in Al-based high-entropy alloys TiHfNbTaAl and TaNbHfZrAl](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.25048)
*Junjin Huang,Wenbo Sun,Longfu Li,Shuangyue Wang,Jingjun Qin,Rui Chen,Zaichen Xiang,Yucheng Li,Lingyong Zeng,Huixia Luo*

Main category: cond-mat.supr-con

TL;DR: This paper reports two new high entropy alloy (HEA) superconductors, TaNbHfZrAl and TiHfNbTaAl, both with VEC=4.2 and BCC structure, exhibiting bulk type-II superconductivity with Tc of 5.5 K and 3.2 K respectively. The study shows that Tc depends on VEC and elemental composition, and Al incorporation leads to more crystalline-like Tc behavior.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: HEA superconductors, first reported in 2014, are of interest due to their high disorder, serving as model systems to study materials intermediate between crystalline and amorphous states. This work aims to characterize new HEA superconductors and understand how VEC and composition influence their superconducting properties.

Method: Two HEAs (TaNbHfZrAl and TiHfNbTaAl) with VEC=4.2 and BCC structure were synthesized. Superconductivity was characterized via resistivity, magnetic, and specific heat measurements.

Result: Both samples are bulk type-II superconductors. TaNbHfZrAl has Tc=5.5 K; TiHfNbTaAl has Tc=3.2 K. Tc correlates with VEC and elemental composition. Al addition induces more crystalline-like Tc dependence in highly disordered HEA superconductors.

Conclusion: The VEC and elemental composition are key factors influencing Tc in HEA superconductors. Introducing Al into highly disordered HEAs shifts their behavior toward more crystalline-like Tc dependence, providing insight into the disorder-property relationship in these intermediate-state materials.

Abstract: Since the first report of a high entropy alloy (HEA) superconductor in 2014, HEAs have continued to captivate the interest of superconducting researchers. Owing to the significant degree of disorder inherent in these systems, they serve as exemplary models for examining the properties of materials that exist in states intermediate between crystalline and amorphous structures. Here we present the superconductivity properties and crystal structure of TaNbHfZrAl and TiHfNbTaAl HEAs, which both have the VEC of 4.2 and body centered cubic (BCC) structure. Through resistivity, magnetic, and specific heat measurements, we prove that both samples are the bulk type-II superconductors with a critical temperature Tc of 5.5 K for TaNbHfZrAl and Tc of 3.2 K for TiHfNbTaAl. The Tc of HEA superconductors is influenced by the VEC and the element composition. And the incorporation of Al in high disorder HEA superconductors causes a more crystallinelike Tc dependence.

</details>


### [122] [Light-Driven Intrinsic Perfect Superconducting Diode Effect](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.25197)
*Makoto Ichikawa,Youichi Yanase*

Main category: cond-mat.supr-con
